10	## PL-01: Method check
11	**Entry:** `src/rllab/train/pipelines/pl01_check.py::run_check` · **Trigger:** `rllab check <config.toml>`; also PL-03 rows 2–5
12	**Covers:** TRAIN-06
13	
14	| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
15	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
16	| 1 | `load_run_configs` | train.config | config path, seeds → list[RunConfig] | `tomllib` into frozen dataclasses, method defaults filled, name `<stem>-s<seed>` | TRAIN-01 |
17	| 2 | `check_plan` | train.check | list[RunConfig] → MethodConfig per method | each distinct team method with `check_settings` applied | TRAIN-06 |
18	| 3 | `train_checks` | train.check | MethodConfig per method, seed → Learner per method | make the method's check world and learner, step it for the fixed check budget | TRAIN-06 |
19	| 4 | `CheckDiscrete` | worlds.checks | overrides → CheckWorld | 6-state corridor, exits left of 0 (0.8) and right of 5 (1.0), γ = 0.9 (AD-09) | TRAIN-06 |
20	| 5 | `CheckContinuous` | worlds.checks | overrides → CheckWorld | 1-D point, reward −distance to the target | TRAIN-06 |
21	| 6 | `score_checks` | train.check | Learner per method, MethodConfig per method → list[CheckResult] | share of `states()` where `policy().act` matches `best_actions()` | TRAIN-06 |
22	
23	### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
24	```python
25	def run_check(config_path: Path, seed: int = 0) -> list[CheckResult]:
26	    configs = load_run_configs(config_path, [seed])
27	    plan = check_plan(configs)
28	    learners = train_checks(plan, seed)
29	    return score_checks(learners, plan)
30	```

35	**Covers:** TRAIN-01–TRAIN-04, TRAIN-07, LOG-01, LOG-02, CHECK-01, WORLD-01–WORLD-07, SNAKE-01–SNAKE-06, FISH-01–FISH-08, FISH-11, PREY-01–PREY-06, PREY-11, QR-01, QR-03, QR-06

41	| 3 | `FishTank` | worlds.fish | variant, overrides → World | "one" variant: bouncing ball, smooth pushes, respawn on hit | FISH-01–FISH-08 |
42	| 4 | `PredatorPrey` | worlds.prey | overrides → World | fish movement, catches remove prey, pellets without respawn | PREY-01–PREY-06 |

46	| 8 | `train_loop` | train.loop | World, Learner per team, RunConfig, root → RunRecord | steps to the budget, writing the run folder through `runlog.RunWriter`; checkpoints at 0–100%; 20-seed evals at every 5% via `core.play_episode`; stops on `NonFiniteError` | TRAIN-01–TRAIN-04, LOG-01, LOG-02, QR-01, QR-03 |

50	def run_train(config: RunConfig, root: Path) -> RunRecord:

52	    learners = make_learners(config, world.spec)

57	## PL-03: Training batch
58	**Entry:** `src/rllab/train/pipelines/pl03_batch.py::run_batch` · **Trigger:** `rllab batch <config.toml> --seeds 1-5` (and `rllab train`, one seed)
59	**Covers:** TRAIN-05, TRAIN-06, QR-02
60	
61	| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
62	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
63	| 1 | `load_run_configs` | train.config | config path, seeds → list[RunConfig] | PL-01 as built | TRAIN-01 |
64	| 2 | `check_plan` | train.check | list[RunConfig] → MethodConfig per method | PL-01 as built | TRAIN-06 |
65	| 3 | `train_checks` | train.check | MethodConfig per method, seed → Learner per method | PL-01 as built | TRAIN-06 |
66	| 4 | `score_checks` | train.check | Learner per method, MethodConfig per method → list[CheckResult] | PL-01 as built | TRAIN-06 |
67	| 5 | `require_pass` | train.check | list[CheckResult] → list[CheckResult] | raise `CheckFailedError` on the first failure | TRAIN-06 |
68	| 6 | `run_parallel` | train.batch | list[RunConfig], root, workers → list[RunRecord] | `spawn` process pool, one `run_train` (PL-02 as built) per config, one torch thread each (AD-05) | TRAIN-05, QR-02 |
69	
70	### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
71	```python
72	def run_batch(config_path: Path, seeds: Sequence[int], root: Path, *, workers: int | None = None, check_seed: int = 0) -> list[RunRecord]:
73	    configs = load_run_configs(config_path, seeds)
74	    plan = check_plan(configs)
75	    learners = train_checks(plan, check_seed)
76	    results = score_checks(learners, plan)
77	    require_pass(results)
78	    return run_parallel(configs, root, workers)
79	```

105	| 3 | `RandomPolicy` | baselines.random | ActionSpace → Policy | uniform draws from the passed `rng` | EVAL-01 |

138	## PL-08: Replay video
139	**Entry:** `src/rllab/cli/pipelines/pl08_video.py::run_video` · **Trigger:** `rllab video <world> <variant> <side> --seed N --out FILE [--sight]`
140	**Covers:** REPLAY-07, REPLAY-02, QR-06
141	
142	| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
143	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
144	| 1 | `open_sources` | replay.sources | world, variant, side spec → Checkpoint or baseline per team | as `open_sides`, one side | REPLAY-07 |
145	| 2 | `make` | worlds.make | world, variant → World | PL-02 as built | WORLD-06 |
146	| 3 | `policies_for` | replay.sources | Checkpoint or baseline per team, WorldSpec → Policy per team | `methods.load_policy` or `baselines` per team; a team left out takes the first checkpoint's | REPLAY-01, REPLAY-06 |
147	| 4 | `play_episode` | core.episode | World, Policy per team, seed → Episode | reset, act per team, step until done or `max_steps`, frames kept | REPLAY-02, WORLD-02 |
148	| 5 | `to_trace` | replay.tracing | Episode, WorldSpec, label → Trace | copy frames, set label and step-limit flag | REPLAY-02 |
149	| 6 | `save_video` | replay.video | Trace, out → Output | draw each frame off-screen, imageio-ffmpeg H.264 at 30 fps | REPLAY-07, QR-06 |
150	
151	### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
152	```python
153	def run_video(world: str, variant: str, side: str, seed: int, out: Path, *, overlay: bool = False) -> Output:
154	    sources = open_sources(world, variant, side)
155	    w = make(world, variant)
156	    policies = policies_for(sources, w.spec)
157	    episode = play_episode(w, policies, seed, trace=True)
158	    trace = to_trace(episode, w.spec, side)
159	    return save_video(trace, out, overlay_sight=overlay)
160	```

163	### core.ActionSpace: type
164	`ActionSpace = Discrete(n: int) | Box(dims: int, low: float, high: float)`, both frozen dataclasses
165	- **Invariants:** `Discrete` actions are ints in `[0, n)`; `Box` actions are `dims` floats in `[low, high]`
166	- **Boundary:** worlds, baselines, methods, refcheck · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.ActionSpace/ · **Cases:** snake `Discrete(3)`; fish `Box(2, -1.0, 1.0)`
167	- **Used by:** worlds, baselines, methods, refcheck
168	
169	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:15 -->
170	### core.TeamSpec: type
171	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class TeamSpec: name: str; agents: tuple[int, ...]; score_name: str; direction: Direction`
172	- **Invariants:** `agents` are indices into the world's agent axis; teams of one world are disjoint and cover every agent
173	- **Boundary:** worlds, train, evaluate, charts · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.TeamSpec/ · **Cases:** snake's one team; predator-prey's prey (6) and predator (2) teams
174	- **Used by:** every component reading scores
175	
176	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:21 -->
177	### core.WorldSpec: type
178	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class WorldSpec: world: str; variant: str; agents: tuple[str, ...]; teams: tuple[TeamSpec, ...]; obs_len: int; action: ActionSpace; max_steps: int; measures: tuple[MeasureSpec, ...]; sight: float | None`
179	`MeasureSpec(name: str, team: str, direction: Direction)`
180	- **Invariants:** available without running an episode (WORLD-04); `sight` is the radius in world units for local-sight variants, else `None`; equality of two specs is field by field
181	- **Boundary:** worlds, methods, runlog, replay, refcheck · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.WorldSpec/ · **Cases:** snake default; fish local-sight (8 agents, sight set); predator-prey
182	- **Used by:** all components; PL-02, PL-08
183	
184	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:28 -->
185	### core.Step: type
186	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Step: obs: Array; rewards: Array; active: Array; done: bool; truncated: bool`
187	- **Invariants:** `obs` float32 `n_agents × obs_len`; `rewards` float32 `n_agents`; `active` bool `n_agents`, and an inactive agent's row is zeros with reward 0 (WORLD edge case); `truncated` is true only when `max_steps` ended the episode
188	- **Boundary:** worlds, methods, refcheck · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Step/ · **Cases:** a snake mid-episode step; a predator-prey step with one prey caught
189	- **Used by:** core.play_episode, train, refcheck
190	
191	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:34 -->
192	### core.EpisodeResult: type
193	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class EpisodeResult: seed: int; length: int; scores: Mapping[str, float]; team_rewards: Mapping[str, float]; measures: Mapping[str, float]`
194	- **Invariants:** `scores` and `team_rewards` keyed by team name (WORLD-05, LOG-02); `measures` keyed by every `MeasureSpec.name`
195	- **Boundary:** train, runlog, evaluate, refcheck · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.EpisodeResult/ · **Cases:** a snake episode with 12 food; a predator-prey episode with both team scores
196	- **Used by:** train, runlog, evaluate, refcheck
197	
198	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:40 -->
199	### core.DrawState: type
200	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class DrawState: width: float; height: float; objects: tuple[Drawable, ...]`
201	`Drawable(kind: str, x: float, y: float, size: float, team: str | None, agent: int | None)`
202	- **Invariants:** lists every object on screen at that step (WORLD-06); snake cells use grid coordinates with `size` 1; `agent` set for agents, so the viewer can draw `WorldSpec.sight` around them
203	- **Boundary:** worlds, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.DrawState/ · **Cases:** a snake board; an 8-fish tank with the ball
204	- **Used by:** replay

206	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:47 -->
207	### core.Frame: type
208	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Frame: step: int; draw: DrawState; scores: Mapping[str, float]; rewards: Array`
209	- **Invariants:** `scores` is each team's running score after this step; `rewards` the latest per agent (REPLAY-05); frame 0 is the reset state with zero rewards
210	- **Boundary:** replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Frame/ · **Cases:** frame 0 and frame 1 of seed 7 snake
211	- **Used by:** replay
212	
213	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:53 -->
214	### core.Episode: type
215	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Episode: result: EpisodeResult; frames: tuple[Frame, ...] | None; hit_step_limit: bool`
216	- **Invariants:** `frames` is set only when traced, with `result.length + 1` entries
217	- **Boundary:** train, evaluate, replay, refcheck · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Episode/ · **Cases:** an untraced snake episode; a traced 3-step check-world episode
218	- **Used by:** train, evaluate, replay, refcheck; PL-08

220	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:59 -->
221	### core.World: type
222	```python
223	class World(Protocol):
224	    spec: WorldSpec
225	    def reset(self, seed: int) -> Step: ...
226	    def step(self, actions: Array) -> Step: ...
227	    def result(self) -> EpisodeResult: ...
228	    def draw(self) -> DrawState: ...
229	```
230	- **Invariants:** same seed → same start (WORLD-01); same seed and actions → same steps (WORLD-02); `actions` has one row per agent, rows of inactive agents ignored
231	- **Errors:** `step` raises `ActionError(agent: str, value: object)`, a `ValueError`, for an action outside `spec.action`, never clipping it; `step` after `done` raises `RuntimeError`
232	- **Boundary:** train, evaluate, replay, refcheck · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.World/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a recorded 5-step snake episode from seed 42
233	- **Used by:** every component that plays episodes; PL-02, PL-08
234	
235	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:73 -->
236	### core.Policy: type
237	`class Policy(Protocol): def act(self, obs: Array, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Array`
238	- **Invariants:** `obs` is `k × obs_len` for the `k` agents of one team; returns `k` actions inside the world's `ActionSpace`; deterministic for a given `obs` and `rng` state
239	- **Boundary:** baselines, methods, evaluate, replay, refcheck · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Policy/ · **Cases:** a replay double of the snake rule policy over 5 observations
240	- **Used by:** core.play_episode, train, evaluate, replay, refcheck; PL-08
241	
242	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:79 -->
243	### core.play_episode: The one episode loop
244	`def play_episode(world: World, policies: Mapping[str, Policy], seed: int, *, trace: bool = False) -> Episode`
245	- **Invariants:** `policies` keyed by team; the seed spawns the world's and each policy's generator (AD-05), so the same inputs give the same `Episode`; stops at `done` or `spec.max_steps`
246	- **Errors:** `KeyError` naming a team with no policy; `ActionError` propagates from `World.step`
247	- **Boundary:** train, evaluate, replay, refcheck · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.play_episode/ · **Cases:** snake rule policy, seed 7, traced
248	- **Used by:** train, evaluate, replay, refcheck; PL-02, PL-08

338	### methods.Learner: type
339	```python
340	class Learner(Protocol):
341	    def act(self, obs: Array, rng: np.random.Generator) -> Array: ...   # exploring
342	    def observe(self, t: Transition) -> None: ...                       # updates on its own schedule
343	    def policy(self) -> Policy: ...                                      # frozen, deterministic copy
344	    def state_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]: ...
345	```
346	- **Invariants:** hand-written learning code only (CHECK-01); `policy()` is greedy (DQN) or the mean action (PPO) and does not change as the learner trains
347	- **Errors:** `observe` raises `NonFiniteError(value: float)` when a loss or parameter is NaN or infinite, before changing any parameter
348	- **Boundary:** train · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/methods.Learner/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a DQN learner over 10 snake transitions
349	- **Used by:** train; PL-01, PL-02, PL-03

384	`def run_check(config_path: Path, seed: int = 0) -> list[CheckResult]`
385	- **Invariants:** one result per distinct team method in the config, trained on its check world (`check-discrete` for `dqn`, `check-continuous` for `ppo`) with `check_settings`, for the fixed check budget; under a minute on the laptop (TRAIN-06, AD-09)

389	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:215 -->
390	### train.run_train: One training run
391	`def run_train(config: RunConfig, root: Path) -> RunRecord`
392	- **Invariants:** exactly `budget` world steps (TRAIN-01); checkpoints at 0%, 10%, … 100% (TRAIN-03); evaluation of every team on `EVAL_SEEDS` at every 5% (TRAIN-04); same config on the same machine → identical logs and checkpoints (TRAIN-02)
393	- **Errors:** `RunExistsError` before any work if the run folder exists; `NonFiniteError` ends the run as `failed` with step and value, keeping the last good checkpoint; interruption marks it `incomplete`
394	- **Boundary:** PL-03 · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/train.run_train/ · **Cases:** a fake writing the finished 3-checkpoint snake run folder
395	- **Used by:** train.run_batch; PL-02, PL-03

397	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:222 -->
398	### train.run_batch: Training batch
399	`def run_batch(config_path: Path, seeds: Sequence[int], root: Path, *, workers: int | None = None, check_seed: int = 0) -> list[RunRecord]`
400	- **Invariants:** loads one config per seed, runs the method check with `check_seed` before any run, then one process per config up to `workers` (default: the core count), each with one torch thread (TRAIN-05, TRAIN-06, AD-05)
401	- **Errors:** `CheckFailedError(result: CheckResult)` before any run starts; a failing run doesn't stop the others
402	- **Boundary:** cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/train.run_batch/ · **Cases:** a fake returning 2 finished snake records
403	- **Used by:** cli; PL-03

500	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Comparison: baseline: Literal["random", "rule"]; team: str; learned_mean: float; baseline_mean: float; difference: float; p_raw: float; p_holm: float; rejected: bool`
501	- **Invariants:** `difference` is signed so positive means better in the team's direction

505	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:329 -->
506	### evaluate.compare: Paired permutation test
507	`def compare(learned: Array, baseline: Array, direction: Direction, *, permutations: int = 10_000, rng: np.random.Generator) -> tuple[float, float]`
508	- **Invariants:** `learned` is runs × seeds, averaged over runs per seed; `baseline` has one score per seed, on the same seeds; swaps labels within each seed; returns (difference, one-sided p) as EVAL-04 states; built and checked with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill
509	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if seed counts differ
510	- **Used by:** evaluate.final_evaluation

512	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:335 -->
513	### evaluate.Summary: type
514	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Summary: world: str; variant: str; team: str; runs: tuple[str, ...]; learned_mean: float; learned_range: tuple[float, float]; run_means: Mapping[str, float]; random_mean: float; rule_mean: float; comparisons: tuple[Comparison, ...]; verdict: Verdict; collapse: int | None`
515	`Verdict(beats_baselines: bool, failing_runs: tuple[str, ...], runs_needed: int)`
516	- **Invariants:** one per world, variant and team (EVAL-06, PREY-08); `comparisons` empty and `runs_needed > 0` with fewer than 5 runs; `collapse` is set when the team's in-training eval score stays at or below its random-baseline mean for the last 4+ consecutive eval points, and is the step of the first of them
517	- **Boundary:** charts, refcheck, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/evaluate.Summary/ · **Cases:** a passing snake summary; one with a failing run; one with 3 runs
518	- **Used by:** refcheck, cli; PL-06

520	<!-- docs/packages/rllab/contract.md:342 -->
521	### evaluate.final_evaluation: Final evaluation
522	`def final_evaluation(runs: Sequence[Run], out: Path, *, workers: int | None = None) -> list[Summary]`
523	- **Invariants:** plays every final checkpoint and both baselines on `FINAL_SEEDS` (EVAL-03); Holm across the 2 comparisons per team; a pass needs both nulls rejected (EVAL-04) and every run's mean beating the rule mean (EVAL-05); in predator-prey each team plays against the rule-based opponent (PREY-08); within 10 minutes (QR-05)
524	- **Boundary:** refcheck, cli · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/evaluate.final_evaluation/ · **Cases:** a fake returning the passing snake summary
525	- **Used by:** refcheck, cli; PL-06

57	Run by the reviewer agent over what it is given (base, package, tasks, paths): the interface review after Phase 0, a phase review per package at each checkpoint, and the milestone exit. `dt land` already checked each landing's `Writes:`, protected paths and gates; review judges what tools can't.

59	**Interface** (after Phase 0): the code is the contract, no more and no less.
60	1. Types match `contract.md`: fields, types, invariants. Nothing a step's In → Out needs is missing; no helper is frozen.
61	2. Signatures match the step tables, in their owner modules. Each stub names the open task that owns its step; no logic beyond types and validation.
62	3. One sample per case the contract names, valid and realistically shaped, never carrying a TC's expected value. Replay doubles replay an interaction the Protocol allows; fakes serve their fixtures.
63	4. `__init__.py` exports exactly the contract; each `Mitigated by` in the plan's dependency graph exists; each split task's tester can write its tests from the frozen interface and its `Read:`.
64	
65	**Each phase**, per package, tests before code:
66	1. **Interfaces followed** (CR-09): no frozen type, signature or contract folder changed outside a Revise. Consumers use the declared types: no dict for a dataclass, no reaching into a provider's internals, no local redefinition. A stub is replaced only by the task it names; no cross-package import outside the contract; spine tests run on samples and doubles, not another step's code.
67	2. **Tests prove something:** each intent test asserts its TC case or acceptance bullet, loading expected values and tolerances from fixtures; none is assertion-free or weakened; Hypothesis runs the `ci` profile. Unit tests are specific, never computing the expected value with the code under test or testing only a mock. **Mutation check** (`.claude/agents/reviewer.md`): per acceptance bullet, the smallest change that should break it; a mutation no test catches is a finding.
68	3. **Correct to the spec:** each acceptance bullet truly met, not special-cased to the sample; the error paths the requirement names; seeding and determinism; numerical edge cases.
69	4. **Simple** (CR-11, CR-12, CR-42): no abstraction with one implementation outside the contract or an AD; no forwarding wrapper, one-method class, or class where a function does; no parameter, flag or config key every caller sets the same; no `try/except` that only re-raises or swallows, no checks the types already guarantee; no loop the library already provides. No near-copies within or across the phase's tasks: one concept is one function in its owner module (tests may repeat for readability). Each finding names the simpler form, with the same tests passing.
70	5. **Pipelines** (CR-10): entry functions read as their step tables (`dt layout`); step owners respected; spine baselines still selectable.
71	6. **The bar** (CR-07): diff this file, `pyproject.toml`, `scripts/` and `.claude/agents/` against the milestone's Base. Any loosening is Critical; new exceptions need an owner and expiry.
72	7. **Hidden and parallel risks:** where a real provider could act differently from its double (flagged for the system phase); fixed output paths, global state, test-order dependence.
73	8. **The rest:** readability, security, performance where a QR sets a number; reused reference or probe code has its Caveats handled and fits the contract; `dt lint` clean, pre-build sections replaced by `dt layout` pointers.
74	
75	**Milestone exit**, over the whole diff, adds: the Scope's Exit holds; every Covers ID has a test; `check.sh full --exit` finds no stub; system tests use real providers; the same sample through a real provider and its double agrees; deferred requirements untouched; pip-audit ran.
76	
77	**Severity:** Critical: a frozen interface changed, a weakened test, an intent test that misses a mutation changing its TC's expected output, a wrong answer, a loosened bar, a security hole, lost data; it stops the build. Important: a consumer bypassing a type, any other surviving mutation, an unneeded abstraction, a duplicate across tasks. Suggestion and Nit: local. Findings go under the checkpoint block in `tasks/plan.md` (`dt finding`), each with a fix task or a reason.

2	# One entry point for every quality check. Hooks call it; so do CI and people.
3	# Written by dev-team2 build-setup. Change it through build-setup (Revise), not by hand:
4	# CONSTRAINTS.md names the rule each step enforces.
5	#
6	#   scripts/check.sh fast FILE...        per edit, instant               (PostToolUse: tester, implementer)
7	#   scripts/check.sh collect             tester's finish gate            (Stop: tester)
8	#   scripts/check.sh gate [--pkg ID]     implementer's finish gate       (Stop: implementer and solo; dt land)
9	#   scripts/check.sh full [--pkg ID]     checkpoints, CI                 (reviewer)
10	#   scripts/check.sh full --exit         the milestone exit: also no interface stub left
11	#   scripts/check.sh protected           print the protected-path globs  (path_guard.sh deny --protected)
12	#
13	# The base is refs/worktree/checks-base, one per worktree (dt claim sets it in a builder's worktree,
14	# dt land in the main checkout), else HEAD.
15	#
16	# tests/contracts/** in the protected list means the boundary contracts frozen at the base (CR-09);
17	# a contract folder new since the base belongs to the interface task freezing it, which may write it,
18	# and so does a frozen folder the task's .claude/.task_writes names (a re-freeze interface task).
19	#
20	# collect runs the intent tests changed since the base and fails unless they are red for the right
21	# reason (scripts/red_check.py): AssertionError or an interface stub's NotImplementedError, never
22	# an ImportError or AttributeError.
23	#
24	# --pkg ID runs the tests of package ID and of every package that depends on it (multi-package:
25	# deptry too); gate and full also run tests/contracts, whole, when it exists. Lint, types, size
26	# and boundaries always cover the whole repo; they take seconds.
27	# FILE paths are absolute or relative to the project root.
28	# Prints every failure (it does not stop at the first) and exits 1 if any step failed.

34	# The protected list (CONSTRAINTS.md, "Roles and protected paths"). One list serves as git
35	# pathspecs here and as path_guard.sh globs; in both, * also matches /.
36	PROTECTED=('tests/intent/**' 'tests/fixtures/**' 'tests/conftest.py' 'pyproject.toml' 'uv.lock' 'CONSTRAINTS.md' 'scripts/**' '.claude/agents/**' '.claude/settings.json' 'references/**' 'probes/**' 'tests/contracts/**')

41	# A package (or, in a single package, a component) and every package that depends on it,
42	# from the architecture's dependency rules.
43	pkg_scope() {
44	  case "$1" in
45	    core) echo "core worlds baselines methods train runlog charts evaluate replay refcheck cli" ;;
46	    worlds) echo "worlds baselines train charts evaluate replay refcheck cli" ;;
47	    baselines) echo "baselines train charts evaluate replay refcheck cli" ;;
48	    methods) echo "methods train charts evaluate replay refcheck cli" ;;
49	    train) echo "train refcheck cli" ;;
50	    runlog) echo "runlog methods train charts evaluate replay refcheck cli" ;;
51	    charts) echo "charts cli" ;;
52	    evaluate) echo "evaluate charts refcheck cli" ;;
53	    replay) echo "replay cli" ;;
54	    refcheck) echo "refcheck cli" ;;
55	    notes) echo "notes cli" ;;
56	    cli) echo "cli" ;;
57	    # The package itself: its tests sit under tests/{intent,unit}/rllab/, so this covers every component.
58	    rllab) echo "rllab" ;;
59	    *) return 1 ;;
60	  esac
61	}

63	# Non-Python parts (CONSTRAINTS.md names them): their own check commands, run in gate and full.
64	other_parts() {
65	  : # e.g. step npm --prefix web run check
66	}

69	mode="${1:-gate}"
70	shift || true
71	pkg=""
72	exit_check=()
73	args=()
74	while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do

77	    --exit) exit_check=(--exit); shift ;;

90	# A step that is not a Python tool (a script, git, another toolchain).
91	step() {
92	  echo "-> $*" >&2
93	  "$@" || { rc=1; failed+=("$*"); }
94	}

96	# A tool from the project's locked environment, never whatever is first on PATH.
97	run() {
98	  echo "-> $*" >&2
99	  uv run --frozen --quiet "$@" || { rc=1; failed+=("$*"); }
100	}

102	# A measured check that fails only at the milestone exit: it prints its findings, and the reviewer
103	# records them, but a mid-milestone run passes.
104	report() {
105	  echo "-> $* (reported; fails only with --exit)" >&2
106	  uv run --frozen --quiet "$@" || echo "(reported, not failing: $*)" >&2
107	}
108	
109	# pytest exits 5 when it collects nothing. That is fine for gate and full on a project with no
110	# tests yet; collect calls pytest through run() so an empty tests/intent fails.
111	run_tests() {
112	  echo "-> pytest $*" >&2
113	  uv run --frozen --quiet pytest "$@"
114	  local status=$?
115	  [ "$status" -eq 5 ] && { echo "(no tests collected)" >&2; return; }
116	  [ "$status" -eq 0 ] || { rc=1; failed+=("pytest $*"); }
117	}

119	# The commit this task started from: refs/worktree/checks-base, set per worktree by `dt claim` (and
120	# by `dt land` in the main checkout). Without it, HEAD, which misses changes already committed.
121	BASE_REF="refs/worktree/checks-base"
122	base_commit() {
123	  git rev-parse -q --verify "$BASE_REF" 2>/dev/null || git rev-parse -q --verify HEAD 2>/dev/null
124	}
125	
126	# A boundary contract is frozen once its folder exists at the base (CR-09): each folder under
127	# tests/contracts/ at the base, and any file directly in it. A folder new since the base is the
128	# interface task freezing it, so that task may write it; the next task's base freezes it. A frozen
129	# folder the task's .claude/.task_writes names (from its Writes:, by dt claim) is a re-freeze
130	# interface task's, so it is open to that task; dt land checks the Writes: against the plan.
131	CONTRACTS_GLOB="tests/contracts/**"
132	claimed_contract() {
133	  local line
134	  [ -s .claude/.task_writes ] || return 1
135	  while IFS= read -r line; do
136	    line="${line%%[[:space:]]}"
137	    line="${line%/}"; line="${line%/\*\*}"
138	    [ "$line" = "$1" ] && return 0
139	  done <.claude/.task_writes
140	  return 1
141	}
142	frozen_contracts() {
143	  local base meta path
144	  base=$(base_commit) || return 0
145	  git ls-tree "$base" -- tests/contracts/ 2>/dev/null | while IFS=$'\t' read -r meta path; do
146	    case "$meta" in
147	      *" tree "*) claimed_contract "$path" || echo "$path/**" ;;
148	      *) echo "$path" ;;
149	    esac
150	  done
151	}
152	
153	# The protected list as enforced: PROTECTED, with tests/contracts/** narrowed to the frozen contracts.
154	protected_globs() {
155	  local g
156	  for g in "${PROTECTED[@]}"; do
157	    if [ "$g" = "$CONTRACTS_GLOB" ]; then frozen_contracts; else echo "$g"; fi
158	  done
159	}
160	
161	# Fail if anything matching the given pathspecs changed since the base, untracked files included.
162	unchanged_since_base() {
163	  local label="$1" base new
164	  shift
165	  echo "-> $label unchanged since $(git rev-parse -q --verify "$BASE_REF" >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "$BASE_REF" || echo HEAD)" >&2
166	  if ! base=$(base_commit); then
167	    echo "no commit to compare against: commit the tester's work first" >&2
168	    rc=1; failed+=("$label: no commit"); return
169	  fi
170	  git --no-pager diff --exit-code --stat "$base" -- "$@" >&2 || { rc=1; failed+=("$label changed"); }
171	  new=$(git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -- "$@")
172	  if [ -n "$new" ]; then
173	    printf 'new untracked files in %s:\n%s\n' "$label" "$new" >&2
174	    rc=1; failed+=("$label changed")
175	  fi
176	}
177	
178	# The tester may change tests/intent only. Docs and plans are the plugin's business, not this check's.
179	only_intent_changed() {
180	  local specs=(tests "${SRC_DIRS[@]}" "${PROTECTED[@]}")
181	  [ -n "$PACKAGES_DIR" ] && specs+=("$PACKAGES_DIR")
182	  unchanged_since_base "files outside tests/intent" "${specs[@]}" ':(exclude)tests/intent'
183	}
184	
185	# The intent test files the tester changed since the base, committed or not.
186	changed_intent_tests() {
187	  local base
188	  base=$(base_commit) || return 0
189	  { git diff --relative --name-only --diff-filter=d "$base" -- 'tests/intent/*.py'
190	    git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -- 'tests/intent/*.py'; } | sort -u |
191	    while IFS= read -r f; do case "${f##*/}" in test_*|*_test.py) echo "$f" ;; esac; done
192	}
193	
194	# Test directories for this run: all of them, or the --pkg scope; tests/contracts always whole.
195	test_dirs() {
196	  local scope="" d p
197	  [ -n "$pkg" ] && scope=$(pkg_scope "$pkg")
198	  for d in tests/intent tests/unit; do
199	    if [ -z "$scope" ]; then
200	      [ -d "$d" ] && echo "$d"
201	    else
202	      for p in $scope; do [ -d "$d/$p" ] && echo "$d/$p"; done
203	    fi
204	  done
205	  [ -d tests/contracts ] && echo tests/contracts
206	}
207	
208	# deptry checks what each package imports against what it declares.
209	dependencies() {
210	  local p scope
211	  if [ -z "$PACKAGES_DIR" ]; then
212	    run deptry . --no-ansi
213	    return
214	  fi
215	  if [ -n "$pkg" ]; then
216	    scope=$(pkg_scope "$pkg")
217	  else
218	    scope=$(cd "$PACKAGES_DIR" && for d in */pyproject.toml; do [ -f "$d" ] && dirname "$d"; done)
219	  fi
220	  for p in $scope; do
221	    echo "-> deptry $PACKAGES_DIR/$p" >&2
222	    (cd "$PACKAGES_DIR/$p" && uv run --frozen --quiet deptry . --no-ansi) || { rc=1; failed+=("deptry $PACKAGES_DIR/$p"); }
223	  done
224	}
225	
226	static_checks() {
227	  run ruff check --ignore-noqa .   # exceptions go in per-file-ignores, which is protected (CR-01)
228	  run ruff format --check .
229	  run "$TYPE_GATE"
230	  run python scripts/check_size.py "${SRC_DIRS[@]}"
231	  dependencies
232	  run lint-imports --no-logo
233	}
234	
235	case "$mode" in
236	  fast)
237	    # Only Python files, only the files just edited. Fixes what it can in place.
238	    files=()
239	    for f in ${args[@]+"${args[@]}"}; do [[ $f == *.py ]] && files+=("$f"); done
240	    [ ${#files[@]} -eq 0 ] && exit 0
241	    run ruff check --fix --quiet "${files[@]}"
242	    run ruff format --quiet "${files[@]}"
243	    run typos "${files[@]}"
244	    run python scripts/check_size.py "${files[@]}"
245	    ;;
246	  collect)
247	    if [ ! -d tests/intent ]; then
248	      echo "tests/intent does not exist: write the intent tests there" >&2
249	      exit 1
250	    fi
251	    run ruff check --ignore-noqa tests/intent
252	    run ruff format --check tests/intent
253	    run typos tests/intent
254	    run pytest tests/intent --collect-only -q --no-cov
255	    changed=()
256	    while IFS= read -r f; do changed+=("$f"); done < <(changed_intent_tests)
257	    run python scripts/red_check.py ${changed[@]+"${changed[@]}"}
258	    step bash scripts/floor_guard.sh
259	    only_intent_changed
260	    ;;
261	  gate)
262	    static_checks
263	    other_parts
264	    dirs=$(test_dirs)
265	    # shellcheck disable=SC2086  # one directory per word, by construction
266	    [ -n "$dirs" ] && run_tests $dirs --no-cov -q -x
267	    step bash scripts/floor_guard.sh
268	    protected=()
269	    while IFS= read -r g; do protected+=("$g"); done < <(protected_globs)
270	    unchanged_since_base "protected files" "${protected[@]}"
271	    ;;
272	  full)
273	    static_checks
274	    other_parts
275	    if [ "$TYPE_GATE" = mypy ]; then run pyright; else run mypy; fi
276	    # CR-41: the interface task's stubs and fields stay unused until later tasks use them.
277	    if [ ${#exit_check[@]} -gt 0 ]; then run vulture; else report vulture; fi
278	    run pylint --disable=all --enable=duplicate-code --recursive=y --score=n --fail-under=10 "${SRC_DIRS[@]}"   # CR-42
279	    run typos
280	    run pip-audit --skip-editable   # the external opinion: a vulnerability database (CR-26)
281	    step bash scripts/floor_guard.sh ${exit_check[@]+"${exit_check[@]}"}
282	    dirs=$(test_dirs)
283	    # Coverage threshold comes from [tool.coverage.report] fail_under (CR-40).
284	    # shellcheck disable=SC2086
285	    [ -n "$dirs" ] && HYPOTHESIS_PROFILE=ci run_tests $dirs --cov -q
286	    ;;
287	  protected)
288	    protected_globs
289	    ;;
290	  *)
291	    echo "unknown mode: $mode (fast | collect | gate | full | protected)" >&2
292	    exit 64
293	    ;;
294	esac
295	[ $rc -ne 0 ] && printf 'FAILED: %s\n' "${failed[@]}" >&2
296	exit $rc

1	# RL Lab — Architecture
2	
3	**Status:** Approved · **Shape:** Single package · **Last updated:** 2026-10-17 · **Spec:** [spec.md](spec.md)
4	
5	Single package (`rllab`): one learner, one laptop, one install, nothing deployed. The one seam that could become its own package is `refcheck`, the only component that carries Stable-Baselines3 and Gymnasium. It sits behind an optional `check` extra, so a split would move it out as it is.
6	
7	## Overview
8	Every world implements one multi-agent `World` protocol (AD-04): it resets from a seed, steps on one action array for all agents, and reports per-team scores and a drawable state. `train` drives a hand-written learner per team (AD-03) over a world for a fixed step budget. It plays held-out evaluation episodes through `core.play_episode` and writes everything to a run folder through `runlog` (AD-06). `evaluate` replays final checkpoints and both baselines on the final seeds and runs the paired permutation test. `charts` draws learning curves from run folders. `replay` re-simulates any checkpoint or baseline from a seed into a `Trace`, then shows it in a pygame window or writes it to MP4 (AD-07). `refcheck` trains Stable-Baselines3 on the same world through an adapter, kept away from every training run (AD-08).
9	
10	```mermaid
11	flowchart LR
12	  cli --> train & evaluate & charts & replay & refcheck & notes
13	  train --> methods & runlog & worlds & baselines
14	  evaluate --> methods & runlog & worlds & baselines
15	  replay --> methods & runlog & worlds & baselines
16	  refcheck --> evaluate & train & worlds
17	  charts --> runlog & evaluate
18	  methods --> runlog
19	  worlds & baselines & methods & runlog --> core
20	```
21	
22	## Tech stack
23	| Area | Choice | Decision |
24	|---|---|---|
25	| Language, env | Python 3.12, uv, one `pyproject.toml` | AD-01 |
26	| Networks, autograd | PyTorch ≥ 2.5, CPU wheels; `nn` layers and `optim` only | AD-02 |
27	| Numerics, world physics | NumPy ≥ 2.1, vectorised over agents | AD-04 |
28	| Learning methods | Hand-written DQN (discrete) and PPO (continuous), one learner per team | AD-03 |
29	| Seeds, parallel runs | `np.random.SeedSequence`, one spawned process per run | AD-05 |
30	| Run storage | JSON record + JSON Lines logs + `torch.save` checkpoints per run folder | AD-06 |
31	| Replay window, video | pygame-ce ≥ 2.5; imageio ≥ 2.36 with imageio-ffmpeg ≥ 0.5 (bundled ffmpeg) | AD-07 |
32	| Charts | matplotlib ≥ 3.9, PNG + SVG | — |
33	| Reference check | stable-baselines3 ≥ 2.4 + gymnasium ≥ 1.0, optional extra `check` | AD-08 |
34	| Settings | TOML files in `configs/`, read with `tomllib` into frozen dataclasses | — |
35	| CLI | `argparse`, one `rllab` console script | — |
36	| Tests, import rules | pytest; import-linter | — |
37	
38	## Components
39	| Component | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on |
40	|---|---|---|---|
41	| `core` | World and policy protocols, shared types, held-out seed sets, and the one episode loop every other component plays through | WORLD-01–WORLD-07 (interface), WORLD edge cases | — |
42	| `worlds` | Snake, the three fish-tank variants, predator-prey and the two check worlds, behind `worlds.make` | SNAKE-01–SNAKE-06, FISH-01–FISH-08, PREY-01–PREY-06, TRAIN-06 (check worlds) | `core` |
43	| `baselines` | Random play for any action format, each world's hand-written rule acting on observations only, and their mean scores on the evaluation seeds | EVAL-01, EVAL-02, SNAKE-07, FISH-09, PREY-07 | `core`, `worlds` |
44	| `methods` | Hand-written DQN and PPO learners behind `Learner`; rebuilds a frozen `Policy` from a checkpoint | CHECK-01, TRAIN-07 | `core`, `runlog` |
45	| `train` | One run over the step budget: per-team learners, checkpoints, in-training evaluations, invalid-number stop; batches across cores; the check-world gate | TRAIN-01–TRAIN-07, QR-01–QR-03, TRAIN edge cases | `core`, `worlds`, `baselines`, `methods`, `runlog` |
46	| `runlog` | Run folders: record, episode and evaluation logs, checkpoints, live progress, run lookup | LOG-01, LOG-02, LOG-06, TRAIN-03 (storage), TRAIN edge cases (overwrite, incomplete) | `core` |
47	| `charts` | Learning curves with range bands and baseline lines, extra measures, two-team curves, pairing heatmaps; follows the `dataviz` skill | LOG-03–LOG-05, PREY-10, PREY-09 (chart), LOG edge cases | `runlog`, `evaluate` |
48	| `evaluate` | Final evaluation on the final seeds, paired permutation tests run with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill, verdicts, summaries, fixed-opponent team evaluation, pairing grids, collapse flags | EVAL-03–EVAL-06, SNAKE-08, FISH-10, PREY-08, PREY-09, QR-05, EVAL and PREY edge cases | `core`, `worlds`, `baselines`, `methods`, `runlog` |
49	| `replay` | Opens replay sides, re-simulates checkpoints or baselines into a `Trace`; pygame viewer with pause, step, speeds, side by side and sight overlay, testable headless; MP4 export | REPLAY-01–REPLAY-08, QR-04, REPLAY edge cases | `core`, `worlds`, `baselines`, `methods`, `runlog` |
50	| `refcheck` | Trains and evaluates Stable-Baselines3 DQN/PPO on a world through a Gymnasium/VecEnv adapter, and compares with the hand-written summary | CHECK-02, CHECK-03, CHECK edge case | `core`, `worlds`, `train`, `evaluate`, `runlog` |
51	| `notes` | Write-up scaffold with the three headings only, and the finish check (parts present, struggle link, broken links) | NOTES-01–NOTES-03, NOTES edge case | — |
52	| `cli` | The `rllab` command: dispatches each trigger to its pipeline entry and prints the result; owns no step (AD-10) | LOG-06 (view), REPLAY-01 (entry) | `core`, `worlds`, `baselines`, `methods`, `train`, `runlog`, `charts`, `evaluate`, `replay`, `refcheck`, `notes` |
53	
54	## Interfaces
55	See [packages/rllab/contract.md](packages/rllab/contract.md).
56	
57	## Data
58	No external datasets: every world is simulated. Generated data lives under `runs/<world>/<variant>/<run-name>/` (AD-06):
59	- `run.json`: the LOG-01 record, the run's config and its status (`running`, `finished`, `incomplete`, `failed` with step and value).
60	- `episodes.jsonl`: one line per training episode (LOG-02). `evals.jsonl`: one line per 5% evaluation point (TRAIN-04), flushed as written so `rllab watch` reads it live (LOG-06).
61	- `checkpoints/ckpt_<pct>.pt`: 11 checkpoints, every 10% (TRAIN-03), each holding the `WorldSpec`, per-team method configs and state dicts.
62	- Final evaluations under `runs/<world>/<variant>/_eval/<date>/` (`summary.json`, per-episode scores), reference runs under `_reference/`, charts and videos under `reports/<world>/`, write-ups at `notes/<world>.md`.
63	
64	Seeds come from three disjoint ranges (AD-05): training episode seeds drawn by the run's generator from [0, 10⁶), the 20 in-training evaluation seeds 1,000,000–1,000,019, and the 100 final seeds 2,000,000–2,000,099, shared by every run and baseline.
65	
66	## Cross-cutting concerns
67	- **Determinism (WORLD-02, TRAIN-02, REPLAY-02, FISH-11, PREY-11):** no global random state. Each episode seed spawns the world's and the policies' generators, and torch runs single-threaded with deterministic algorithms (AD-05). A committed digest of a short snake run's logs and final checkpoint is checked in CI. A later world that changes it fails the build (FISH-11, PREY-11), and so does a fish digest once S2 lands.
68	- **No outside RL code in training (CHECK-01):** import-linter forbids `stable_baselines3` and `gymnasium` outside `refcheck`, and a test asserts neither is in `sys.modules` after a training run (AD-08).
69	- **Speed (QR-01, QR-02, QR-04, QR-05):** world physics is NumPy over all agents at once, learners batch every team's observations into one forward pass, and evaluation spreads seeds over processes. `rllab` logs each run's wall time against the 60-minute budget, and a timing test plays a 1,000-step replay headless.
70	- **GPU (QR-03):** `device` is a run setting, `cpu` by default; a rented GPU run uses the same code, and its cost goes in the write-up by hand.
71	- **Offline (QR-06):** nothing imports a network client. ffmpeg comes bundled with imageio-ffmpeg, and fonts come from pygame's built-in one.
72	- **Outputs:** every step that writes a chart, video or write-up returns a `core.Output` naming the file and what it was made from, so a write-up can cite it (NOTES-02).
73	- **Errors:** bad actions raise `core.ActionError` naming the agent and value (never clipped). Spec mismatches on checkpoint load raise `methods.SpecMismatchError` naming the field. Mixed-settings charts raise `charts.MixedRunsError` naming the differing settings.
74	- **Skills:** `charts` follows `dataviz` for every chart (LOG-04). `evaluate.compare` is built and checked with `null-hypothesis-testing`, whose inputs are its signature (EVAL-04).
75	
76	## Project layout
77	```
78	src/rllab/     → core/ worlds/ baselines/ methods/ train/ runlog/ charts/ evaluate/ replay/ refcheck/ notes/ cli/ — one folder per component; __init__ exports its contract
79	                 train/pipelines/ holds the PL-01–PL-03 entries, cli/pipelines/ the PL-04–PL-12 entries (AD-10)
80	configs/       → one TOML per world and variant: budget, per-team method settings
81	runs/          → generated run folders (git-ignored)
82	reports/       → charts and videos kept alongside the write-ups
83	notes/         → one write-up per world, the learner's own words
84	tests/         → folders only; files are planned in pipelines.md
85	```
86	
87	## Decisions
88	### AD-01: Python 3.12 in one uv-managed package
89	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
90	- **Context:** One learner on an Apple Silicon laptop; PyTorch, pygame-ce and Stable-Baselines3 all ship arm64 macOS wheels for Python. QR-06 needs the whole install to work offline once set up.
91	- **Decision:** Python 3.12, one `rllab` package with a `uv.lock`. Stable-Baselines3 and Gymnasium go in an optional `check` extra.
92	- **Alternatives:** Python 3.13: wheels for some libraries lag. Separate `rllab-check` package: a real boundary only if the reference stack becomes a burden, so it is a named seam for now.
93	- **Consequences:** `requires-python = ">=3.12,<3.13"`. Training can run without the `check` extra installed, which proves CHECK-01 at install level too.
94	
95	### AD-02: PyTorch for networks and gradients, not for the learning algorithm
96	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
97	- **Context:** CHECK-01 allows automatic gradient computation but the learner writes action choice, learning targets and updates. Networks are small MLPs on observations of tens of numbers (WORLD-03). Training is CPU-only (QR-01).
98	- **Decision:** Learners use `torch.nn` layers, `torch.optim` optimisers and autograd. Replay buffers, exploration, targets, advantage estimation, clipping and update schedules are hand-written in `methods`.
99	- **Alternatives:** NumPy with hand-derived backprop: teaches backprop, which is not this project's subject, and is slower to debug. JAX: capable, but less familiar CPU tooling and a functional style that hides the update loop.
100	- **Consequences:** `torch.set_num_threads(1)` per run process (AD-05); small batches stay fast on CPU. The PyTorch install is about 200 MB.
101	
102	### AD-03: DQN for discrete actions, PPO for continuous ones, one learner per team
103	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
104	- **Context:** Snake has 3 discrete actions (SNAKE-02); fish and predator-prey push with two numbers in [−1, 1] (FISH-02, PREY-02). Many-fish has 8 agents (FISH-06), and predator-prey has two teams learning at once (TRAIN-07). The brief wants each world to teach one idea, and wants checking against a reference implementation (CHECK-02).
105	- **Decision:** `dqn` (replay buffer, target network, ε-greedy, Huber loss) for snake and the discrete check world. `ppo` (Gaussian policy, GAE, clipped objective) for fish, predator-prey and the continuous check world. All agents of a team share one learner (parameter sharing over a batch of agents). Teams have separate learners in the same run. Evaluation and replay act deterministically: greedy for DQN, the mean action for PPO.
106	- **Alternatives:** PPO everywhere: one method fewer, but snake would not teach value learning. SAC for fish: strong on continuous control, but a third method and harder to explain for many agents. Tabular Q-learning for snake: SNAKE-06 includes length, an unbounded number, so the table would need discretising.
107	- **Consequences:** Two methods to write, each with a check world (TRAIN-06) and a Stable-Baselines3 equivalent (AD-08). A prey that leaves the episode is masked out of its team's batch.
108	
109	### AD-04: Our own multi-agent World protocol, not Gymnasium or PettingZoo
110	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
111	- **Context:** WORLD-01–WORLD-07 need seeded resets, a declared spec, team-labelled agents, per-team scores with a direction, and a drawable state. The brief bets that one core serves a grid game, smooth movement and two teams.
112	- **Decision:** `core.World` is a small protocol. Observations, actions, rewards and active masks are NumPy arrays indexed by agent, teams map to agent indices in `WorldSpec`, and `draw()` returns a `DrawState`. "Not seen" entries are a 0/1 flag followed by zeros, so lengths stay fixed (WORLD-03, FISH-07, PREY-06).
113	- **Alternatives:** Gymnasium: single-agent, with no teams, score direction or drawable state, so all of those would be extras. PettingZoo parallel API: multi-agent, but dict-per-agent stepping is slow in Python and adds a dependency the learner didn't write.
114	- **Consequences:** `refcheck` writes the one Gymnasium/VecEnv adapter (AD-08). Every world implements all five methods, and `core.play_episode` is the only episode loop.
115	
116	### AD-05: Seeds from SeedSequence, disjoint held-out ranges, one process per run
117	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
118	- **Context:** WORLD-01, WORLD-02, TRAIN-02 and REPLAY-02 need exact repeats; TRAIN-04 and EVAL-03 need seeds never used in training; TRAIN-05 and QR-02 need runs in parallel, one per core.
119	- **Decision:** A run seed spawns the learner's, exploration and episode-seed generators through `np.random.SeedSequence`. An episode seed spawns the world's and the policies' generators inside `core.play_episode`. Training draws episode seeds from [0, 10⁶); evaluation seeds are the fixed `core.EVAL_SEEDS` and `core.FINAL_SEEDS` ranges (see Data). `train.run_batch` runs each config in its own `spawn` process with `torch.set_num_threads(1)` and `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True)`.
120	- **Alternatives:** Global `np.random.seed`/`torch.manual_seed`: order-dependent, breaks once evaluation interleaves with training. Threads: the GIL serialises the Python world loop.
121	- **Consequences:** Identical runs are promised on the same machine only (TRAIN-02). In-training evaluation never touches the training generators, so turning evaluation off for a test leaves training identical.
122	
123	### AD-06: Run folders of JSON, JSON Lines and torch checkpoints
124	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
125	- **Context:** LOG-01 and LOG-02 need a record and per-episode logs. LOG-06 needs them readable while the run writes, at most one evaluation point behind. A stopped run keeps its checkpoints and is marked incomplete. A run name that already exists is refused (TRAIN edge cases). REPLAY edge cases need a checkpoint to carry its world spec.
126	- **Decision:** One folder per run (see Data). Logs are append-only JSON Lines, flushed at every evaluation point; `run.json` is rewritten atomically (write, then rename) on each status change. A checkpoint is a `torch.save` dict of spec, method configs and state dicts, loaded with `weights_only=True`.
127	- **Alternatives:** Parquet: fast to read but not appendable while a run is live. SQLite: one file for everything, but concurrent writers from parallel runs need care and the files are no longer readable by eye.
128	- **Consequences:** Charts and evaluation read whole JSONL files; at ~10⁴ episodes per run that is instant. A crashed process leaves `run.json` at `running`; `runlog` reports any `running` run with no live process as incomplete.
129	
130	### AD-07: Replays re-simulate into a Trace, shown with pygame-ce and saved with imageio-ffmpeg
131	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
132	- **Context:** REPLAY-01–REPLAY-08 need any checkpoint or baseline from any seed, step back, 4 speeds, side by side, a sight overlay and video export. QR-04 needs ≥ 30 steps/s; QR-06 needs it offline. Worlds are deterministic (AD-05), so nothing needs recording during training.
133	- **Decision:** `replay.trace_sides` (or, for one side, `to_trace` over a traced `core.play_episode`) plays the whole episode headless through `core.play_episode` into a `Trace` of frames (at most the world's `max_steps`), then the viewer only draws frames. Stepping back is an index change. 1× is 30 steps/s. pygame-ce draws circles, cells and text; MP4 (H.264) export goes through imageio's ffmpeg writer.
134	- **Alternatives:** matplotlib animation: slow at 16× for 8 fish and clumsy keyboard control. A browser viewer: smooth, but a local web server and JavaScript stack for one user (spec: everything local).
135	- **Consequences:** The `Trace` is testable without a window, so REPLAY-02 is a frame comparison. Memory is one episode of `DrawState`s, under 1,000 frames of a few dozen objects.
136	
137	### AD-08: Stable-Baselines3 as the reference, isolated in `refcheck`
138	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
139	- **Context:** CHECK-02 needs an established implementation of the same kind of method (DQN, PPO; AD-03) trained on the same world, budget and seeds; CHECK-01 forbids it inside training runs. Worlds speak `core.World`, not Gymnasium (AD-04).
140	- **Decision:** `refcheck` wraps a world as a Gymnasium env (single agent) or as an SB3 `VecEnv` with one slot per agent of the learning team (many-fish: 8 slots stepping one world, matching parameter sharing). It trains SB3 `DQN` or `PPO` for 5 seeds and evaluates on `core.FINAL_SEEDS` through `core.play_episode`. In predator-prey, SB3 cannot co-train two models, so each team's reference trains against the rule-based opponent, the same opponent PREY-08 evaluates against.
141	- **Alternatives:** CleanRL single-file scripts: easy to read, but they would need editing to fit our worlds, which weakens them as a reference. RLlib: multi-agent native, but a heavy distributed stack for a laptop.
142	- **Consequences:** SB3's default hyperparameters differ from ours; `refcheck` starts from the hand-written method's settings where SB3 has the same knob. The predator-prey reference answers a narrower question than co-training, so CHECK-03's 80% bar compares a fixed-opponent reference with a co-trained team there; the learner accepted this (2026-10-17).
143	
144	### AD-09: Every batch first runs the method's check world
145	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
146	- **Context:** TRAIN-06 needs every learning method to learn a hand-derived best behaviour before it is used in a world. Bugs in RL are silent (brief), and methods change while a world is built.
147	- **Decision:** `train.run_check` trains each team method in a config on its check world: for DQN a 6-state corridor with two exits, left of state 0 (reward 0.8) and right of state 5 (reward 1.0), γ = 0.9, so the best action is left in states 0–1 and right in 2–5 and a learner biased to one action can't pass; for PPO a 1-D point that is rewarded for reaching a target, where the best push is the sign of the offset. It needs ≥ 95% agreement over every check-world state. `train.run_batch` runs the same check steps with the batch's method settings before starting any run and refuses the batch on failure. `rllab check <config>` runs it alone.
148	- **Alternatives:** A recorded pass per method version: saves seconds but goes stale silently when code changes. A test only: passes in CI but not for the settings actually used.
149	- **Consequences:** Each batch spends under a minute on the check. A check-world settings override exists because a world's tuned settings (e.g. buffer size) may be too large for a 6-state world.
150	
151	### AD-10: Pipeline entries are the public API; `cli` only dispatches
152	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
153	- **Context:** Pipeline design gave every workflow one entry function calling named steps (PL-01–PL-12). Entries import their steps' owners, so where an entry lives sets an import direction. PL-03 runs PL-02 in worker processes, so `train` can't depend on `cli`. A chart, a summary and a heatmap each need two or more components that the `cli` row already depends on.
154	- **Decision:** `train` hosts the PL-01–PL-03 entries (`run_check`, `run_train`, `run_batch`) and exports them as its contract. Every other entry lives in `cli/pipelines/`. Entries return contract types. `cli/__main__.py` parses arguments, loads configs through `train.load_run_configs`, calls the entry, prints its result and sets the exit code; `cli` owns no pipeline step.
155	- **Alternatives:** All entries in `cli`: the batch's worker processes would import `cli`, inverting the dependency. Each entry in its main component: `charts` and `evaluate` entries would need `runlog` and `baselines` steps, which they may do, but the replay and refcheck entries would need printing in their own components.
156	- **Consequences:** The contract carries the train entries, the config loader and every step function a `cli` entry calls. `cli` has no step tests; its intent tests drive `main` with arguments.
157	
158	### AD-11: Stay a single package past the size triggers
159	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
160	- **Context:** The contract passed its 200-line size ceiling and the package has 12 pipelines, past the ~3-pipeline Grow trigger. The learner chose to stay single (2026-10-17). Nothing runs or deploys on its own, and the one dependency stack the rest shouldn't carry (Stable-Baselines3, Gymnasium) is already isolated as the optional `check` extra in `refcheck` (AD-08).
161	- **Decision:** `rllab` stays one package. Size is handled by `dt context`, which loads only the contract sections and pipelines a task reads, and import-linter keeps the component directions.
162	- **Alternatives:** Split `refcheck` into its own package: a real boundary, but already met by the extra. Split the shared core from the worlds: every world needs the core, and the core has no other consumer.
163	- **Consequences:** The Grow triggers stay reported by `dt` and are answered by this decision. A new trigger (a part that runs on its own, a heavy new stack) reopens it.
164	
165	## Risks and open questions
166	- **Many-fish and predator-prey speed (QR-01, QR-02).** 8–10 agents, 1,000-step episodes and 21 × 20 evaluation episodes may not fit 60 minutes in Python. Mitigation: NumPy physics over all agents, evaluation in a worker process, and a timing check in the first fish build; QR-03's GPU route doesn't help a CPU-bound world loop.
167	- **5 parallel runs on mixed cores (QR-02).** Apple Silicon's efficiency cores run slower; 5 runs may not all land on performance cores. Measured in the first snake batch.
168

10-- **Type sections.** Every type one step hands to another, and every type a public signature takes or returns, is its own section with the title `type`: `### profile.Profile: type`. The first line is its shape as code: a frozen dataclass's fields, a `typing.Protocol`, or a pandera schema's columns for a dataframe. Then **Invariants** and **Used by**. `pipeline-design` names these types in every step's `In → Out`; the build's interface task writes them, as code, into the owner's `types.py`. Freeze only what crosses: a helper's or a private function's types stay out.
11-- **Contract first:** the contract is defined before either side is built. **The provider owns it.**
12:- **Boundaries.** A symbol consumed across owners adds one line: `- **Boundary:** <consumers> · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/<id>.<symbol>/ · **Cases:** <the payloads a fixture must show>`. Every type that crosses step owners has one, components included: consumers are packages, components, or both. This section is the boundary's source of truth. `build-planning`'s interface task freezes it into that folder: a schema (JSON Schema across runtimes; a pandera schema for a dataframe in Python), one sample per case, and, for a function a pipeline calls from another pipeline or package, a fixture-backed fake of the provider. A Protocol or class type's case is a replay double (a fixture-backed recording of the real object's calls, `a recorded 5-batch epoch`), and so is a function another component's step calls: it is the type's sample, not a fake. Both sides then build at once against the same samples. A type needs at least one case (`dt lint` `type-cases`). Name the cases; don't write the fixtures. A case may name a probe's redacted sample (`a posted purchase (PB-01 samples/sync_added.json#0)`); the interface task derives the type's sample from it, never copies it.
13-- **Public only, ≤ ~8 lines per symbol.** Helpers, modules and call order belong in the package's architecture or its `pipelines.md`. A retired symbol collapses to one line under `## Retired`: `- ~~profile.foo: Title~~ Retired YYYY-MM-DD: reason`.
14-
15-````markdown
16-# [pkg] — Contract
17-
18-**Package:** [pkg] · **Status:** Draft · **Last updated:** YYYY-MM-DD · **Architecture:** [../../architecture.md](../../architecture.md)
19-
20-## profile
21-
22-### profile.Profile: type
23-`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Profile: coefs: np.ndarray; periods: tuple[float, ...]; residuals: np.ndarray`
24-- **Invariants:** `coefs` 2×K for K `periods` (a sine and a cosine each); `residuals` has one entry per hour, at least 168
25:- **Boundary:** forecast · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/profile.Profile/ · **Cases:** a planted daily sine over 4 synthetic weeks
26-- **Used by:** forecast
27-
28-### profile.fit_seasonality: Seasonal profile fit
29-`def fit_seasonality(demand: Demand, *, terms: Terms = "daily+weekly") -> Profile`
30-- **Invariants:** one sine/cosine pair per harmonic; `residuals` has the input's length and mean zero
31-- **Errors:** `ValueError` if there are fewer than 168 hours or the input contains NaN
32-- **Used by:** forecast
33-````

1	# Milestone shape: interface, step spines, wiring, system
2	
3	Read when writing a milestone's tasks. Examples: `examples-phase0.md`.
4	
5	A milestone is planned from its pipelines' steps: each step has one owner, one module and typed `In → Out`, so it is the unit of work. The plan freezes every boundary between steps first, so the work on both sides starts at once instead of queueing behind one spine. Every task carries `**Kind:**`:
6	
7	```
8	## Phase 0: Interface              interface (solo, first)       types, signatures, samples, fakes, exports, __main__
9	## Phase 1: Step spines            spine, one per step           each writes its owner module only
10	## Phase 2: Wiring and thicken     wiring, one per pipeline      its entry module, smoke test
11	                                   thicken (tasks.md)            each after its step's spine
12	## Phase 3: System                 system (solo, last)           fakes swapped for real providers
13	```
14	
15	Phases name the usual waves; the dependency graph (`tasks.md` step 9) decides what may start. Tester halves of split tasks start right after the interface.
16	
17	**a. Interface task** (`**Kind:** interface`, `**Step:** contract`, solo, `**Depends on:** —`). It freezes, as code, every boundary in the milestone whose two sides belong to different tasks:
18	- each contract `type` section the milestone's steps pass (`In → Out`), written into its owner's `types.py`, with a schema (pandera for a frame, JSON Schema across runtimes) and one sample per case in `tests/contracts/<component>.<Type>/`. A case naming a probe sample (`PB-01 samples/sync_added.json#0`) puts `PB-01:samples` in `Read:`; the task derives the type's sample from it, never copies it
19	- each new step's signature in its owner module, with the body `raise NotImplementedError("<task id>")` naming the task that replaces it
20	- for each function a pipeline calls from another pipeline or package (its `**Boundary:**` line): a fixture-backed fake serving its fixtures, where the caller's tests load it. A `call` edge to a contract symbol is mitigated by that fake; `none` there needs a reason (`dt lint`)
21	- for each object with behavior a step passes (a Protocol or class: `core.Dataset`, `runstore.RunWriter`) and each call into another component's function: the Protocol or signature plus a fixture-backed replay double in `tests/contracts/<component>.<Type>/`. The double is that type's sample, not a fake: the system task never swaps it
22	- each component's `__init__.py` exports, and the package's `__main__.py`: the dispatch from each pipeline's trigger to its entry function, whose signature it stubs naming the wiring task, in that pipeline's own entry module
23	
24	Not frozen: helpers, private functions, anything one task owns end to end, and boundaries an earlier milestone froze. `Read:` the type and boundary symbols. Acceptance: every sample validates against its type; every signature imports; each fake and replay double serves its fixtures. `Writes:` each component folder as one glob (`src/gridload/profile/**`) plus its contract folders; `task-size` doesn't count them. A milestone across packages may have one interface task per provider, all solo and first; interface tasks never depend on each other. When one runtime's types are generated from another's schema, the providing interface task runs the generator and writes the generated file too (its `Writes:` claims it).
25	
26	**b. Step spines** (`**Kind:** spine`, `**Step:** PL-NN.<function>`, solo, XS or S). One per new step, each depending only on the interface task: it replaces the stub naming it with the step's spine version from the step table. `Writes:` its owner module and that module's unit test, nothing else; steps sharing one module share one spine task. Acceptance: the step's input sample gives a schema-valid output; the signature is unchanged. A step another pipeline provides (`PL-01 as built`) gets no spine.
27	
28	**c. Wiring** (`**Kind:** wiring`, `**Step:** PL-NN`, solo). One per new pipeline: the entry function in the pipeline's own entry module (the layout's, never one module shared by several pipelines), so wiring tasks run side by side, and its smoke test. `__main__.py` is the interface task's. It depends on its pipeline's spines, since the smoke runs them (`order`, `none: integration: …`), never on their thicken tasks. A call to another pipeline or package runs against that boundary's fake, so wiring PL-02 never waits for PL-01. Acceptance: the smoke command runs end to end and is committed as the pipeline's smoke test; the pipeline's pre-build sections are replaced by a `dt layout` pointer (`dt land` runs `dt prebuild-clear` when the wiring lands).
29	
30	**d. System task** (`**Kind:** system`, solo, last). It swaps each fake for the real provider, in the entry its wiring wrote (a `file` edge), and runs every smoke command and the milestone's golden cases on the real chain; the boundary samples still validate against real output. Multi-package: one per new system pipeline, `**Step:**` that pipeline, `**Package:**` the owner of its first step, `**Reason:** system task`; the root has no pre-build sections, so no `dt layout` bullet. Single package: `**Step:**` the calling pipeline. A milestone with no fake has no system task.
31	
32	**Folds** keep the task count down. The pipeline's wiring depends on a fold task, so a fold is allowed only where that task is XS and the critical path doesn't grow. Record each on its task as `**Reason:** fold: <rule>`:
33	- A step with a golden case whose spine and thicken are both in the milestone, or whose spine version is its whole behavior, may be one split thicken task that replaces the stub itself; with a thicken folded in, its acceptance keeps the baseline selectable.
34	- A trivial step (pass-through, print) is built by its pipeline's wiring task; its stub names the wiring task.
35	
36	**Fakes and stubs.** A fake is allowed only where a pipeline calls another pipeline or package: both sides test against the same fixtures, so neither drifts from the contract unseen. Mocking a step is forbidden: a step's spine is the real baseline, and its consumer builds against the type's sample (for a Protocol or class, its replay double). The interface stubs are the floor's one stub carve-out (`build-setup`'s CR-02): allowed while the task they name is open; the gate fails on one naming a done task, the milestone exit on any.
37	
38	**Frozen means frozen.** Changing a frozen type, sample, signature or fake is a `contract-change`: `architecture-design` Revise, then a new interface task naming the symbol in `Read:`, never an edit to make a test pass. The bar enforces it (CR-09): once a contract folder exists at a task's base the implementer can't write it, and `dt guard` asks before any edit no open interface task covers. The re-freeze is that interface task, run by an implementer agent, never the main session: its `Writes:` names the frozen folders it changes, which opens them to it alone.
39

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Diff-scoped floor checks for CONSTRAINTS.md (CR-01 to CR-04, CR-07, CR-08).
3	
4	Called by scripts/floor_guard.sh, which writes the diff, the untracked-file list and the
5	base commit's pyproject.toml. Prints one line per finding and exits 1 if there are any:
6	
7	    path:line: [rule] message
8	
9	Rules (CONSTRAINTS.md ID in brackets):
10	    suppression [CR-01]            a new `type: ignore`, `pyright:`, `noqa`, `pragma: no cover`...
11	    stub [CR-02]                   a function whose body is only pass, ... or NotImplementedError
12	    stub-task [CR-02]              an interface stub, `raise NotImplementedError("M1-T05")`, naming
13	                                   a task that is not open in tasks/todo.md (whole repo, not the diff)
14	    stub-left [CR-02]              any interface stub, with --exit (the milestone exit)
15	    swallowed-exception [CR-02]    `except:` / `except Exception:` with only pass
16	    test-skipped [CR-03]           a new skip, skipif, xfail or importorskip
17	    test-deleted [CR-03]           a test file removed
18	    assertion-removed [CR-03]      a test file lost more assertion lines than it gained
19	    test-without-assertion [CR-03] a changed test function that asserts nothing
20	    secret [CR-04]                 a credential pattern (the value is never printed)
21	    references-import [CR-08]      an import from the references folder
22	    references-path [CR-08]        the references folder put on an import path (sys.path,
23	                                   an environment variable, pytest or type-checker config)
24	    probes-import, probes-path     the same for the probes folder [CR-08]
25	    bar-loosened [CR-07]           pyproject.toml tool config weakened, or a new config file
26	    rule-removed [CR-07]           a CR rule gone from CONSTRAINTS.md
27	    new-exception [CR-07]          a new row in CONSTRAINTS.md's Exceptions table
28	
29	An interface stub is the one placeholder body CR-02 allows: the milestone's interface task writes
30	each step signature that way, naming the task that replaces it, and testers import it before the
31	code exists. It is never a `stub` finding; `stub-task` and `stub-left` judge it instead.
32	
33	A CR-01 to CR-03 or CR-08 finding is allowed when an unexpired row in CONSTRAINTS.md's
34	Exceptions table names that rule and a path glob matching the file. Secrets and CR-07 never are.
35	Files under scripts/ are skipped for code checks: they are the bar, protected separately.
36	Files under references/ and probes/ are inputs, not project code: only the secret check reads them.
37	"""

99	TASK_ID = re.compile(r"M\d+-T\d{2,}")
100	OPEN_TASK = re.compile(r"^\s*- \[ \] (M\d+-T\d{2,})\b", re.MULTILINE)
101	EXEMPT_DECORATORS = {"abstractmethod", "abstractproperty", "overload"}
